642. **A29 outcome: the patent arm (14–15 November 2026).**
- **Publication numbers: 1,394.** 1,307 came from the list runs P1n, P1b, P2a, P2c, P2d, P2f–P2i and P3b, which overlap, and 87 only through the citation, lead and similarity routes P4, P5 and P6. Matched by publication number, so two kinds of one patent (for example CN 119728206 A and B) count twice.
- **Tiers:** T3 0; **T2 1, proposed: CN 122339846 A/B (Guangzhou University, #727), awaiting the user's confirmation under §9**; related (T1) 712; excluded 681. The T2 is of the same kind as Dong et al. (#417), which the user ruled T2 in D35: placement of baits under a resource limit in a power cyber-physical system, by a score from asset value, vulnerability and topology, trained with a reinforcement-learning reward that includes bait cost. It has no zones or conduits, no IEC 62443, no ATT&CK paths and one random baseline, so it narrows A28's claim without threatening it. Two further patents are borderline between T1 and T2 and are counted T1 until the user decides: CN 117675308 A (#838), a game-theoretic honeypot placement along high-harm attack paths with a deployment-cost term, framed for power grids over a generic vulnerability graph; and CN 121012683 A (#886), a bilevel optimisation of how many detection and protection "baits" go in each subgraph of a substation network. Claude's lean on the first is T1 (likely). On the second it is T2 (likely): its Chinese title and claim 1 call it a deception-defence method, its construction is that of Jay 2023 (#305, full-text reading #312), and it has no budget, experiments or citations; D36's first draft leaned T1, and a re-read changed the lean (the text of its entry, #886, has the quotations).
- **Reading:** 262 pages were opened (title, abstract and, for 235, the first independent claim; claims not reached for 27). 41 of them were opened in a second round on 15 November (amendment 6): patents of run P1n with industrial-control, power or security titles that the title rule had excluded or related; 68 in a third round the same day: candidates from runs P1n, P3b and P1b whose titles suggested a strategy, a game, a configuration or an attack path, or whose security, industrial-control or power titles the rule had excluded (29 of them, in runs P1n and P1b); 39 in a fourth round, a last check of the rule's exclusions (patents excluded on titles that named a security topic or that Google had cut, 37 of them from run P1b); and 23 in a fifth round from run P6 (#640; 20 patents new to the data and 3 already in it on a title). The other 1,132: 1,128 were judged on a title alone, 1 on a title and a list snippet, 2 as the family member of a patent that was read, and 1 (US 9,853,999 B2, #347) was read earlier. Each patent whose page was opened has an entry below (#727–#988). How the title screen worked: `01-search-screening-record.md` §14; amendment 6, #643.
- **The closest patents by method** are four, all related (T1) because the tiers gate on the OT domain (#728, #729, #730, #731); one more, #775, is the nearest in a power setting that names an optimisation. The third round added the two borderline calls for the user (#838, #886, above) and two formal placement methods outside OT, CN 118842645 A (#749) and CN 122457351 A (#785). The fourth and fifth rounds added the formal placement methods outside OT that lie nearest to A28's elements: CN 121356815 A (#771), a multi-objective placement with an attack-path blocking term and a criticality-weighted score, and CN 120658528 A (#879), a decision-tree search that maximises the attacker's cost of re-planning; D36 lists the others. D36 sets out what each does and lacks.
- **Tier decisions:** first passes by Claude. On 15 November 2026 the user decided the borderline calls put to him: Cybersense GmbH's US 12,261,855 B2, Bosch's EP 4 586 550 A1, CN 122339820 A, CN 120034354 A and CN 118694612 B all related (T1). Four exclusions whose titles carry a deception-like term and an OT term await his confirmation under §9: CN 116318314 B (#959) and CN 115914014 B (#957), both of Guangzhou Power Supply Bureau, on physical "drone decoy equipment" for substations; CN 117834239 A, a "mimicry" industrial-control firewall (#964); and the design registration CN 304151907 S, an "industrial control honeynet" display model, judged on its title only. The check that found them was run on 15 November, after the second round of reads, on every excluded patent title, with a deliberately wide word match (deception, decoy, lure, bait, trap, mimic, fake, spoof and honey on one side; industrial, power, grid, energy, water, rail, vehicle, control system and similar on the other); it matched the four above and, falsely, honey products with "water" or "electric" in their names. Google Patents cuts long titles in its lists with "…", so the check saw only the visible part of those.
- **Pages that failed:** for 24 of the 262 pages the first fetch returned a server error, at the plain address or at /en, and another address was read; each entry says which. Three pages needed more than that, and all three were read in the end: the A document of CN 119051914 A (#969) failed at both addresses at first, the page of its granted B document, with the same title, was read in its place, and the A document's page was read afterwards at /zh, once the fetch tool was available again (its claims carry no version label, so it is not confirmed whether they are the A or the B text); CN 120498905 A failed at the plain address and at /en and was read at /zh, Google's machine translation of the Chinese (#974); and CN 122621588 A (#981), a cut-title exclusion of run P1b, was refused at its /en address ("robots_disallowed") and was read afterwards at /zh.
- **Record:** every publication number, its runs and its tier: `01-search-screening-record.md` §14–§17.

**Protocol amendments 3 to 7**

643. **Protocol amendments 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 (D36, 14–15 November 2026), with the old and new wording.** `01-search-protocol.md` carries each change in place. The protocol asks that a change be logged with both versions and the reason before the runs it affects are reported; the runs it affects are reported in this same section.
- **Amendment 3, 14 November: the term "honeypoint".** *Before:* block D (§4) had no word for a decoy called a "honeypoint". *After:* §4 notes the gap, and two runs cover it with the five strings unchanged: OpenAlex `search.title_and_abstract=(honeypoint OR honeypoints)` (#618) and the same query on Google Patents (#626). *Why:* one research group calls its decoys "honeypoints", a word block D leaves out. The main search did return one paper with the word in its title (HoneyCenter, #422), so the gap was partial; but BAG-HRF (record 419), also titled with it and among the closest abstracts of this batch, was first found by this run, and none of the main search's five strings returned it. The paper run found 13 new records and the patent run 574 distinct patents.
- **Amendment 4, 15 November: how patents are recorded.** *Before:* §10 gave every kept record its own entry, and patents were not distinguished. *After:* the papers follow §10 as written. For patents, an entry is written for every run and for every patent whose page was opened; the patents judged on a title alone appear only in the screening record's tables (`01-search-screening-record.md` §14–§17), with their runs and tiers. *Why:* the patent runs surfaced 1,394 publication numbers, 1,128 of them judged on a title alone, and an entry for each would have added about 1,128 entries to a log of 612, burying the 262 that were read. *Cost, stated openly:* the main search gave each kept paper an entry even on a title alone, so the two arms are recorded differently. Nothing is lost: every patent number, run and tier is in the screening record. *The user's decision of 15 November 2026 (option a).*
- **S1's narrowing is not an amendment.** §6 already requires it: S1 returned about 24,680 patents, so it was narrowed once (#625) and both runs are recorded.
- **Amendment 5, 15 November: a correction to the protocol's own text.** *Before:* §3's table row for patents and §13's limitation text said patents were searched in "Google Patents and the USPTO's Patent Public Search". *After:* both say Google Patents only. *Why:* §8 specified only Google Patents searches, and no search on the USPTO's own site was run; the wording overstated the protocol. Google Patents covers US grants and applications and the EP, WO, CN, KR and JP documents.
- **Amendment 6, 15 November: how patents were screened.** *Before:* §8 said "Screen each patent on its title, abstract and first independent claim." *After:* patents were screened on their titles by a keyword rule applied on 14–15 November 2026 (`01-search-screening-record.md` §14). A patent whose title suggested deployment or placement, every patent of §8's lead list and the nearest of the citing patents had its page opened and was read on its title, abstract and first independent claim. A second round on 15 November opened 41 more, from run P1n only: every P1n result carries a deception word, a placement word and an OT word in its title, abstract or claims, so a neutral title there can hide a relevant patent, and one of the first nine reads turned one up (CN 122339846 A, proposed T2). A third round the same day opened 68 more: the candidates described above. A fourth round opened 39 more, a last check of the exclusions (`01-search-screening-record.md` §14.4), and a fifth 23 more from the similarity route (amendment 7). 262 patents in all were opened. *Why:* 1,394 patents is far more than a reader can take through three fields each, and the protocol gave no volume for the patent arm (§12 only estimated the papers). *Cost, stated openly:* a placement method behind a neutral title could have been missed among the 1,128 judged on titles alone. Run P1n, the run most likely to hold a neighbour, had 104 of its 260 patents opened; of the 156 that were not, 67 are related on a title only and 89 are excluded on a title, 8 of them with a security-, network-, control- or power-related word in the visible title (false hits, satellite-navigation anti-spoofing and one design registration). Every other patent stands on the title rule (`01-search-screening-record.md` §14.5). The rule's exclusions are not safe: of the 29 excluded titles opened in the third round, 19 proved related (T1) on reading and 10 stayed excluded, and of the 39 opened in the fourth, 26 proved related (T1) and 13 stayed excluded. The thesis limitation says so (§13). The rule was written during the screening, not before it. *Added by Claude beyond the three amendments the user approved; the user can strike it.*
- **Amendment 7, 15 November: a route that starts from a patent (the similarity route, run P6).** *Before:* §8 listed runs P1 to P5 and gave no route that starts from a patent. *After:* §8 adds run P6 (#640). From the Google Patents page of each of the closest patents, every patent and paper named in its "Similar documents", "Patent citations", "Cited by" and "Families citing this family" tables is taken. The patents are screened on their titles by §14's rule; those whose titles suggest deployment or placement, and the power-sector neighbours that the titles leave open, are opened; deception-titled papers are checked against this log. The depth is the nine closest pages (level 1) and the seven pages that level 1 led to (level 2); then it stops. *Why:* CN 121356815 A (#771) showed that a placement method with a stated objective can sit behind a cut title and an OT-free text that no keyword run could return, and the tables are Google's own similarity and citation links, which do not depend on our query words. *Cost, stated openly:* the route gives a sample of machine-chosen neighbours and not a closure; paper rows were judged on titles; patent rows whose titles carry no deception term were counted and not entered; the depth was fixed by Claude and not by saturation; the other formal placement patents read at level 1 had their own tables left untaken (#640). *A gap in the protocol as written, found while doing this:* block O of §4 (industrial control, SCADA, ICS, operational technology, cyber-physical, programmable logic controller, smart grid, substation, critical infrastructure, IIoT) has no word for a power setting: "power grid", "power system", "electric power", "new power system", "distribution network" and "power plant" are missing. Strings S1, S2 and S5 use the block on the paper side, and S1 and the classification run P3b use it on the patent side, so a work that describes a power network in those words and carries no other word of the block is not returned by them. S3, S4, P1b, the assignee, citation and lead runs and P6 do not use the block, and neither do the thirteen earlier rounds of web searching or the forward-citation searches. The text of CN 121356815 A, as read, has no word of the block. The thesis limitation says so (§13), and a widened re-run is offered in D36. *Added by Claude beyond the amendments the user approved; the user can strike it.*
- **Not amendments, stated for completeness:** (1) P2f–P2i searched four large companies on deception terms from the start, so §6's limit was not tested for them (#632); (2) the lead US 12,335,308 (#612) joined §8's list by D35; (3) the classification group's title was verified and recorded (#636), as §8 required.

**Found outside the runs**

644. **Babenko, Kolesnikova, Myrutenko, Kruchinin (2026)** — "Risk-aware proactive hardening of IoT and cyber-physical networks," *Proceedings of the Workshop on Cybersecurity Providing in Information and Telecommunication Systems (CPITS 2026)*, CEUR-WS Vol-4271, paper 5, pp. 65–79 (published 24 September 2026). https://ceur-ws.org/Vol-4271/paper5.pdf 🟢 Free PDF; the abstract, sections 1–3, tables 1–4 and the reference list were read on 15 November 2026, figures not seen. Found incidentally during A17's work, by no run. **T1: related, not deception.** Budget-constrained greedy hardening: each step takes the defence with the largest marginal risk reduction per unit cost, until the budget or a target is reached; the model links CVEs through CWE to ATT&CK techniques and combines CVSS with node criticality and a zone term Z(v) = 1/(1 + d(v)), where d(v) counts zone boundaries from an external entry point. IEC 62443 appears only as a reference for typical industrial architectures: no zones, conduits or security levels as numeric inputs. No deception, honeypots or decoys. Evaluated on synthetic networks of 50–500 nodes. Worth citing next to Sakata et al. (#427) as a budgeted, zone-aware method that is not a deception method.

**Papers kept: tier unresolved (2)**

645. **BAG-HRF: A Bayesian Attack Graph-Based Honeypoint Recommendation Framework** (2025). doi:10.1109/icaisg68699.2025.11452143; OpenAlex W7143459472. A17 record 419: **tier unresolved**, judged on its abstract only (full text not obtained). 🔴 Wang et al., ICAISG 2025 (12 December 2025). Greedy honeypoint placement by marginal risk reduction under a budget, on a Bayesian attack graph with path probabilities and asset values; evaluated on an airport network, not described as OT. The abstract mentions no IEC 62443 zones or conduits, no ATT&CK and no interception-stage term. The full text is closed and no free copy was reached (free-only rule). It is a budgeted, attack-path, asset-valued placement method, so the thesis cites it from its abstract and names it in the limitation. Forward citations: none (#622). Found by: OpenAlex "honeypoint" (#618). *Tier unresolved by the user's decision of 15 November 2026.*

646. **A strategy-making method for pIoT PLC honeypoint defense against attacks based on the time-delay evolutionary game** (2025). doi:10.1109/TIFS.2025.3601442; IEEE Xplore 11217210. A17 record 412: **tier unresolved**, judged on its abstract only (full text not obtained). 🔴 Tan et al., *IEEE Transactions on Information Forensics and Security* 20, 11528–11543. Abstract read on the IEEE page the user saved. A time-delay evolutionary game picks the optimal PLC-honeypoint defence strategy for power-IoT PLCs; the payoff comes from load loss on an IEEE 22-bus test system. It is the one candidate of this batch set in an OT network (power-grid PLCs) with a formal model, and the abstract does not say whether its strategies correspond to which PLCs receive honeypoints. IEEE marks it not open access, and it is in neither OpenAlex nor Semantic Scholar, so no free full text, reference list or citing list was reached (free-only rule). **Tier unresolved by the user's decision of 15 November 2026;** Claude's first pass was T1 (a strategy choice by replicator dynamics, not positions). The thesis cites it from its abstract and names it in the limitation, like HoneyCenter (#422) and TI-DNDP (#420). Found by: the queued backward round from Park & Dagher (#611).

**Patents whose pages were opened**

*Proposed T2 (1).*

727. **CN 122339846 A, "A method and system for active defense of power cyber-physical systems"** (Guangzhou University; priority 2026-06-02; published 2026-07-03; granted as CN 122339846 B on 2026-08-11). Patent, found by P1n. 🟢 Read 15 November 2026 on its Google Patents page through Google's machine translation: **T2, proposed**. **The same kind as Dong et al. (#417), which the user ruled T2 in D35; the user confirms every T2 decision (protocol §9).** An OT/CPS placement method under a resource limit: it takes each node's security state, vulnerability, asset value, type and topological links; picks a target defence set under a defence-resource constraint and deploys baits of matching types (claim 1); claim 3 scores each node's defence benefit from asset value, vulnerability and topology and takes the top scorers up to a preset quantity, claim 4 assigns light, medium or heavy baits by asset value, claim 7 makes the scorer a reinforcement-learning node-level Q-network. Claim 1 also picks safe, bait-free real nodes for operating-system diversity configuration, updates states from attack results and repeats. The description gives the defender's reward as capture bonus minus intrusion penalty minus bait deployment cost, and an attacker who chooses targets by a Softmax over asset value, availability, degree centrality and neighbour value. The setting is an electric power information physical system (servers, switches, gateways, power terminals). The network is an undirected graph: no zones, conduits, IEC 62443 or Purdue; no MITRE ATT&CK, attack paths or attack graph. Its one comparison is with a random strategy; no network, node counts or numeric results are given in the text. The granted document, CN 122339846 B (granted 2026-08-11), has the same claim 1 and the same dependent claims 2 to 7. The same applicant holds CN 122339820 A (#728) and CN 121603304 B (#775). Read on 15 November 2026 through Google's fetch tool on the A page (claims, then the description's reward, attacker model, experiments and citations) and on the B page (claim 1 and a check of which topics the claims cover). https://patents.google.com/patent/CN122339846A/en

*Closest by method (4).*

728. **CN 122339820 A, "An optimization method for honeypoint deployment strategy in a two-layer IoT architecture"** (Guangzhou University; priority 2026-05-12; application, pending (published 2026-07-03)). Patent, found by P1b. 🟢 Read 15 November 2026 on its Google Patents page through Google's machine translation: **T1**. **Closest by method; related (T1) by the OT gate, a judgment call; related (T1) by the user's decision of 15 November 2026.** Honeypoint placement in a two-layer IoT architecture as a zero-sum Stackelberg game over a network layer and a physical layer treated as separate security domains. Strategy sets are grown by oracles; the defender's commitment is a k-sparse optimal-commitment mixed-integer program that maximises expected interception over active attack paths, with exact honeypot counts per layer and node risk scores. No industrial, CPS or power wording; no asset-criticality scoring, IEC 62443 zones or conduits, or ATT&CK; no evaluation reported. Read through machine translation with the formulas stripped, so the exact objective could not be checked. https://patents.google.com/patent/CN122339820A/en

729. **CN 120034354 A, "Honeypot deployment method, device, equipment, medium and product based on attack and defense benefits"** (Beijing University of Posts and Telecommunications; priority 2025-01-06; application, pending). Patent, found by P3b. 🟢 Read 15 November 2026 on its Google Patents page through Google's machine translation: **T1**. **Closest by method; related (T1) by the OT gate, a judgment call; related (T1) by the user's decision of 15 November 2026.** A Stackelberg game that places honeypots on edges under a total-honeypot budget, with the equilibrium found by linear programs; breach probability per node by logistic regression, damage weighted by node importance, attack paths from entry to target nodes, costs and rewards; simulations against an unnamed conventional method. Enterprise and SDN networks; distribution-grid and critical-infrastructure appear as motivation only. No zones or conduits, ATT&CK or IEC 62443. Read through machine translation. https://patents.google.com/patent/CN120034354A/en

730. **US 12,261,855 B2, "Procedures for improving security in an electronic communications network"** (Cybersense GmbH; priority 2021-03-19 (DE 10 2021 106 823.1); 2025-03-25 (granted)). Patent, found by P5. 🟢 Read 14 November 2026 on its Google Patents page: **T1**. Claims: all 5 read; claim 1 only independent. **Closest by method; related (T1) by the OT gate; related (T1) by the user's decision of 15 November 2026.** Builds an attack graph (weak points as sources, the target as sink), sets the type and number of lures and decoys from its structure, and distributes them by a target function that rewards decoys on short paths and on many paths and placements near weak points. The description gives max A*Nsd + B*Nmd + Nrd − Np − E + Mp − Md − Msi − Msd, with A = 10 and B = 2 in the worked example; upper limits on decoy and lure numbers are parameters and deployment complexity is a term (claim 3), but there is no budget. The worked example is an Active Directory graph; industrial controllers appear once, as a possible kind of decoy. No zones, segments or asset criticality. https://patents.google.com/patent/US12261855B2/en

731. **CN 118694612 B, "A honey spot deployment method based on improved multi-objective firefly algorithm"** (State Grid Jiangxi Electric Power (Information and Telecommunication Branch); priority 2024-08-26; granted 2024-12-20). Patent, found by P1b. 🟢 Read 15 November 2026 on its Google Patents page through Google's machine translation: **T1**. **Closest by method in a power setting; related (T1), a judgment call; related (T1) by the user's decision of 15 November 2026.** A power-system network: a MulVAL attack graph, then the top N of M attack edges by CVSS difficulty get real honeypoints. A multi-objective firefly algorithm tunes prediction models of honeypoint positions, and its objectives are prediction errors, not a defensive objective. No budget, asset value or zones; no evaluation. Read through a garbled machine translation of the claims, so the account is less certain than the others. https://patents.google.com/patent/CN118694612A/en

*Guangzhou University and its co-applicants (T1)* (55)

732. **CN 116506213 A, "A server protection method and device based on dynamic IP switching"** (Guangzhou University; priority 2023-05-26; published 2023-07-28; granted as CN 116506213 B on 2025-11-07; active). Patent, found by P1b. 🟢 Read 15 November 2026 on its Google Patents page through Google's machine translation: **T1**. Claims: the opening of claim 1 was quoted; the fetch summary covered the method, any deception wording and any deployment rule. Whitelisted users are sent to the real server at DNS-issued IP addresses that rotate on a schedule; blacklisted users are sent to a honey point server that returns simulated responses and adds attackers to a sub-blacklist; the text calls honeypot technology "a means of deceiving attackers". The example is one server and three honey point servers; no rule for where, how many or which, and no objective, game, budget or asset value. No industrial, power or CPS wording. Patent, found by P1b. 🟢 Read 15 November 2026 on its Google Patents page through Google's machine translation: **T1**. Title translated by Claude from the Chinese original (一种基于白名单文件蜜点的勒索软件防御方法和相关设备); the page shows no English title, and Google Patents' list cut it after "file". Builds a set of file honey points (basic decoy files, ransomware-encryption whitelist files and whitelist decoy files with similar attributes), monitors all system files through the operating system, serialises the access behaviour of a program that touches a honey point and ends the program if it is ransomware. The basic decoys follow a fixed procedure (the first N files in ascending and descending access order, N configurable, for example 5, then mutated); no objective, game, budget, asset value or attack path. General computer systems (Windows event tracing); no industrial wording. The title rule had excluded it because the list cut the title. https://patents.google.com/patent/CN121234365A/en

771. **CN 121356815 A, "A multi-dimensional performance evaluation method and related equipment for honeypoint deployment based on multi-objective optimisation"** (Guangzhou University; priority 2025-09-23; published 2026-01-16; pending (substantive examination requested 2026-02-03)). Patent, found by P1b. 🟢 Read 15 November 2026 on its Google Patents page through Google's machine translation: **T1**. Claims: the opening of claim 1 was quoted; the fetch summary covered the method, any deception wording and any deployment rule. Title translated by Claude from the Chinese original (一种基于多目标优化的多维度效能评估蜜点部署方法及相关设备); Google Patents' list cut it. **The closest patent by method among the IT-side patents read.** It collects network data, builds a MulVAL-based attack graph, analyses penetration paths, scores candidate honeypoint placements on three coverage objectives (area coverage; the attack-path blocking ratio, blocked paths over all paths; vulnerability-ranking coverage) and outputs Pareto-optimal deployment sets with NSGA-II. It chooses where: the number of honeypoints is a cap (within resource limits), not an output, and the other constraints are that nodes be accessible and controllable and that existing services stay stable; no monetary budget. Criticality enters through an environmental influence factor (business importance, asset value and zone level) in the vulnerability score; the network has intranet partitions, service subnets and a DMZ, but no IEC 62443 zones or conduits. The blocked-path ratio is a path term, with no ATT&CK or stage labels beyond "multi-stage penetration path". No OT, ICS or power wording; no experiments or baselines. Against claim A28 it has a stated composite objective (three terms, Pareto), a count cap, partial criticality and a path term; it lacks the OT setting, IEC 62443 zones and conduits as numeric inputs, an ATT&CK-grounded interception stage and baselines. The title rule had excluded it (the list cut the title, which no longer showed a deception term), and run P1n could not have returned it (its OT block has no word that the text uses). Read on three occasions; the readings agree. https://patents.google.com/patent/CN121356815A/en

No explicit budget or cap, no zones, no asset criticality, no ATT&CK. The setting is stated as large-scale power grid systems, but the method itself is a generic vulnerability graph. It is a formal placement method along attack paths with a cost term; it is T1 by the OT gate and T2 only if the power-grid framing is counted as an OT setting. https://patents.google.com/patent/CN117675308A/en

886. **CN 121012683 A, "Method, device, equipment and medium for preventing TSN network spoofing in substations"** (Anhui University of Traditional Chinese Medicine; priority 2025-09-09; published 2025-11-25; pending (substantive examination requested 2025-12-12)). Patent, found by P1n, P3b. 🟢 Read 15 November 2026 on its Google Patents page through Google's machine translation: **T1**. **Borderline T1 or T2, the user's call. Claude's lean is T2, likely; it was T1, unconfirmed, until a re-read on the evening of 15 November (below).** Claim 1 models a substation virtual LAN as a discrete linear dynamic system, builds a strong connection graph of all IEDs, splits it into observable subgraphs (clockwise and counter-clockwise ring graphs plus per-node subgraphs) and deploys "detection baits" and "protection baits" in each subgraph. The decision is a mixed-integer bilevel problem (defender leader, attacker follower): per subgraph, how many attack agents and how many IDS and IPS defence agent groups; utilities come from the per-subgraph attack success probability; it is solved by regret-minimisation decision rules and a unique equilibrium distribution found by Gaussian elimination. No deployment cost, budget, asset criticality, attack-graph or ATT&CK data, zones or conduits; no experiments or baselines; the utility formulas are blank in the machine-translated text. **The re-read** (fetch tool, the page without /en, quotations in the original Chinese). The Chinese title is 一种变电站TSN网络欺骗防御方法、装置、设备及介质, "a substation TSN network deception-defence method, device, equipment and medium"; Google Patents' English title renders 欺骗防御 (deception defence) as "spoofing". Claim 1 opens 一种变电站TSN网络欺骗防御方法, and the background says the invention "introduces deception-defence means that lure attackers away from the real system" (引入了欺骗防御手段，通过诱导攻击者远离真实系统). The protection bait is a target into which attacker traffic is lured, by port mirroring and open virtual switching, and with which the attacker interacts once trapped (攻击者被困在IPS保护诱饵中时，便会与IPS保护诱饵进行交互); the detection bait monitors packets in real time and reads as a sensor; no sentence says a bait imitates a device, and neither is called a honeypot or decoy. The text defines an observable subgraph as one in which every node has an out-degree of at most 1 and builds two rings and, for each node, further subgraphs: the construction of Jay (2023; #312), whose decoy types are likewise protection and detection. The page lists no citations and does not mention Jay or GOOSE; its list of similar documents is automatic, not the applicant's. So the open point, whether the baits are deception assets, resolves towards yes: the patent calls itself deception defence in its title and in claim 1, and lures attackers into one of its two bait types. It is T2 if Jay-type allocation (how many decoys of which type for each observable subgraph) counts as formal deception-placement optimisation in OT, which is how the T2 definition reads: a substation VLAN, a bilevel optimisation, no criticality, no zones. It is T1 if the user does not count that kind of allocation as placement. Counted T1 until he decides. https://patents.google.com/patent/CN121012683A/en

Fine-tunes a language model to map device alarms to ATT&CK tactics and techniques, builds IP behaviour sequences from honeypot alarms, scores the coordination of attacker IPs and prompts a model to infer likely attack paths with probabilities. Honeypoints are the alarm source ("蜜点可以部署在网络外围也可以部署在内网中", they can sit on the perimeter or inside); no rule for where, how many or which, and no objective, game, budget or asset value. The experiment uses a DMZ, a web server and an office zone; the only utility link is the applicant's name. The title rule had excluded it on a neutral title. https://patents.google.com/patent/CN121173601A/en
